)\n380\t\n381\t\n382\tdef _extend_report(\n383\t    base: CapabilityReport,\n384\t    extras: tuple[CapabilityStatus, ...],\n385\t) -> CapabilityReport:\n386\t    \"\"\"Return a new ``CapabilityReport`` with ``extras`` appended and\n387\t    failure-bucket tallies updated.\n388\t\n389\t    The base report's ``blocked_evals`` and ``skip_flags`` are preserved\n390\t    verbatim — \"\"\"Render the stderr HARD-failure artifact per FR-CLI4 AC.\"\"\"\n505\t    by_name = {row.name: row for row in report.report}\n506\t    lines = [\"eval doctor: HARD failures\"]\n507\t    for name _check_claude_version(\n    probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[str]]] = None,\n    min_version: Optional[_VersionTuple] = None,\n    *,\n    config: Optional[EvalConfig] = None,\n) -> CapabilityStatus:\n    \"\"\"HARD check: ``claude --version`` parses and meets the minimum.\n\n    The version floor (R1-mit / T02.20) is sourced from ``EvalConfig`` —\n    there is no in-doctor           name=\"claude.min_version\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"hard\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=\"claude --version not callable\",\n        )\n    parsed = _parse_version(raw)\n    if parsed is None:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"claude.min_version\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"hard\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=f\"could not parse version from {raw!r}\",\n        )\n    passed = parsed >= min_version\n    detail = f\"claude \"\"\"HARD check: ``~/.claude/`` (or override) exists and is a directory.\"\"\"\n    target = home if home is not None else Path.home() / \".claude\"\n    exists = target.is_dir()\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"filesystem.claude_home\",\n        passed=exists,\n        failure_mode=\"hard\",\n        description=\"~/.claude/ directory exists\",\n        detail=str(target) if exists else f\"{target} not found\",\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# NFR-PERF2 free-RAM precheck (T03.17 / D-0059)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n#\n# Design-spec §11 (NFR-PERF2) pins peak resident-set-size at 2.25 GB for\n# ``--parallel 15``. When an operator requests the maximum concurrency on a\n# host that cannot afford that ceiling, the doctor MUST emit a warning\n# string containing the literal ``\"2.25 GB\"`` token so the operator can\n# tell at a glance which budget was insufficient. Hosts with less than\n# 4 GB free RAM are out-of-scope per ``CLAUDE.md`` infrastructure notes —\n# the warning is the documented stop-gap, not an exit-2 hard failure\n# (which would block legitimate ``--parallel 8`` runs on tight hosts).\n\nRAM_CEILING_GB: float = 2.25\n\"\"\"Design-spec §11 NFR-PERF2 peak-RSS ceiling at ``--parallel 15``.\n\nThe literal value ``2.25`` (not the bytes) is the one operators see in\nthe warning text, so changing it requires a roadmap edit. The bytes\nconstant below is derived from it so the two views cannot drift.\n\"\"\"\n\nRAM_CEILING_BYTES: int = int(RAM_CEILING_GB * (1024**3))\n\"\"\"Byte-equivalent of :data:`RAM_CEILING_GB` for free-RAM comparisons.\n\n``int(2.25 * 1024**3) == 2_415_919_104``. Computed once at import time so\nthe helper does not re-derive it on every call.\n\"\"\"\n\nRAM_CEILING_TEXT: str = f\"{RAM_CEILING_GB} GB\"\n\"\"\"Canonical rendering of the ceiling used in operator-facing strings.\n\nThe T03.17 AC asserts that the warning contains this literal token. Pin\nit here so any downstream string formatting picks up changes uniformly.\n\"\"\"\n\nPARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD: int = 15\n\"\"\"``--parallel`` value at-or-above which the RAM precheck fires.\n\nMatches :attr:`RunOrchestrator.MAX_PARALLEL`. Below this threshold the\nbenchmark does not document a ceiling, so the doctor stays silent rather\nthan emit a misleading number.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef _default_free_ram_probe() -> Optional[int]:\n    \"\"\"Return free RAM in bytes via Linux ``/proc/meminfo``.\n\n    Prefers ``MemAvailable`` (kernel ≥3.14) because it accounts for\n    reclaimable cache the way an oncoming allocation would; falls back to\n    ``MemFree`` on ancient kernels. Returns ``None`` on any failure (eg\n    non-Linux host, locked-down container) so the doctor records a\n    ``probe unavailable`` SOFT-SKIP rather than crashing — a doctor that\n    crashes on tight hosts is worse than one that warns.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    meminfo = Path(\"/proc/meminfo\")\n    if not meminfo.is_file():\n        return None\n    try:\n        text = meminfo.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\")\n    except OSError:\n        return None\n    available: Optional[int] = None\n    free: Optional[int] = None\n    for line in text.splitlines():\n        key, _, rest = line.partition(\":\")\n        key = key.strip()\n        if key not in (\"MemAvailable\", \"MemFree\"):\n            continue\n        tokens = rest.strip().split()\n        if not tokens:\n            continue\n        try:\n            kb = int(tokens[0])\n        except ValueError:\n            continue\n        unit = tokens[1].lower() if len(tokens) > 1 else \"kb\"\n        # /proc/meminfo always reports in kB on Linux; the unit check is\n        # defensive in case a future kernel changes the format.\n        multiplier = 1024 if unit == \"kb\" else 1\n        value = kb * multiplier\n        if key == \"MemAvailable\":\n            available = value\n        elif key == \"MemFree\":\n            free = value\n    if available is not None:\n        return available\n    return free\n\n\ndef _check_free_ram_for_parallel(\n    *,\n    requested_parallel: Optional[int],\n    probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n    ceiling_bytes: int = RAM_CEILING_BYTES,\n    threshold_parallel: int = PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD,\n) -> Optional[CapabilityStatus]:\n    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: free RAM is sufficient for the requested concurrency.\n\n    Returns ``None`` (gate inactive) when ``requested_parallel`` is below\n    :data:`PARALLEL_RAM_GATE_THRESHOLD` — the design-spec only pins the\n    RSS ceiling for the upper-bound run, so warning operators about\n    insufficient RAM at ``--parallel 8`` would be noise.\n\n    When the gate is active the helper returns a :class:`CapabilityStatus`\n    whose ``failure_mode`` is ``\"skip\"`` regardless of outcome:\n\n    * Passed when the probe returns a value at or above\n      :data:`RAM_CEILING_BYTES`.\n    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when free RAM is below the ceiling, with the\n      detail string containing the literal :data:`RAM_CEILING_TEXT` token\n      so a grep against the operator-facing artifact pins the failure\n      mode without parsing structure.\n    * Failed (SOFT-SKIP) when the probe returned ``None`` — the host\n      cannot self-report free RAM so we cannot certify the budget either.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if requested_parallel is None or requested_parallel < threshold_parallel:\n        return None\n\n    probe = probe or _default_free_ram_probe\n    description = (\n        f\"free RAM >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n    )\n    try:\n        free_ram = probe()\n    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 — probe is caller-supplied\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe raised {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}; \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget unverified\"\n            ),\n        )\n    if free_ram is None:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free-RAM probe unavailable on this host; cannot verify \"\n                f\"{RAM_CEILING_TEXT} budget\"\n            ),\n        )\n    free_gb = free_ram / (1024**3)\n    if free_ram < ceiling_bytes:\n        return CapabilityStatus(\n            name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n            passed=False,\n            failure_mode=\"skip\",\n            description=description,\n            detail=(\n                f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB < {RAM_CEILING_TEXT} ceiling \"\n                f\"for --parallel {threshold_parallel}\"\n            ),\n        )\n    return CapabilityStatus(\n        name=\"host.free_ram_for_max_parallel\",\n        passed=True,\n        failure_mode=\"skip\",\n        description=description,\n        detail=f\"free RAM {free_gb:.2f} GB >= {RAM_CEILING_TEXT}\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _check_ptytest_vendored(pty_dir: Optional[Path] = None) -> CapabilityStatus:\n    \"\"\"SOFT-SKIP check: ptytest vendored under ``cli/eval/pty/``.\n\n    The actual ptytest vendoring lands at M2; the row stays SOFT-SKIP so\n    doctor still exits 0 on a clean dev machine until the upstream task\n    materialises ``cli/eval/pty/__init__.py``.\n    \"\"\"\n    target = pty_dir if f\"{init_file} not found (vendored at M2)\",\n    )\n\n\ndef _extend_report(\n    base: CapabilityReport,\n    extras: tuple[CapabilityStatus, ...],\n) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Return a new ``CapabilityReport`` with ``extras`` appended and\n    failure-bucket tallies updated.\n\n    The base report's ``blocked_evals`` and ``skip_flags`` are preserved\n    verbatim — supplementary doctor rows only contribute to the three\n    failure buckets (``hard_failures``, ``soft_skips``, ``soft_xfails``).\n    \"\"\"\n    rows = base.report + extras\n    hard_failures = list(base.hard_failures)\n    soft_skips = list(base.soft_skips)\n    soft_xfails = list(base.soft_xfails)\n    for status in extras:\n        if status.passed:\n            continue\n        if status.failure_mode == \"hard\":\n            hard_failures.append(status.name)\n        elif status.failure_mode == \"skip\":\n            soft_skips.append(status.name)\n        else:  # \"xfail\"\n            soft_xfails.append(status.name)\n    return replace(\n        base,\n        report=rows,\n        hard_failures=tuple(hard_failures),\n        soft_skips=tuple(soft_skips),\n        soft_xfails=tuple(soft_xfails),\n    )\n\n\ndef build_doctor_report(\n    skip_flags: Optional[Iterable[str]] = None,\n    *,\n    gates: Optional[CapabilityGates] = None,\n    claude_version_probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[str]]] = None,\n    claude_home: Optional[Path] = None,\n    pty_dir: Optional[Path] = None,\n    config: Optional[EvalConfig] = None,\n    requested_parallel: Optional[int] = None,\n    free_ram_probe: Optional[Callable[[], Optional[int]]] = None,\n) -> CapabilityReport:\n    \"\"\"Aggregate :class:`CapabilityGates` + doctor-specific checks.\n\n    Args:\n \" (xfail)\"\n\n\ndef render_checklist(report: CapabilityReport) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the doctor green-checklist text (FR-CLI4 human output).\"\"\"\n    lines = [\"superclaude eval doctor:\"]\n AC.\"\"\"\n    by_name = {row.name: row for row in report.report}\n    lines = [\"eval doctor: HARD failures\"]\n    for name in report.hard_failures:\n        row = by_name.get(name)\n        if row doctor_payload(\n    report: CapabilityReport,\n    *,\n    check_coverage: bool,\n    coverage_result: Optional[CoverageResult] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Build the ``--json`` payload.\n\n    Extends :meth:`CapabilityReport.to_json` with a ``coverage_gate``\n    marker so callers can detect whether ``--check-coverage`` was passed.\n    The base ``CapabilityReport`` keys are preserved verbatim so the\n    payload remains compatible with the D-0010 contract.\n\n    When ``check_coverage`` is on and ``coverage_result`` is supplied\n    (T04.14 wiring), the marker carries the full :meth:`CoverageResult.to_dict`\n    payload under ``result``; ``status`` is ``\"passed\"`` / ``\"failed\"``\n    based on ``coverage_result.passed``. When ``check_coverage`` is on\n    but no suite was supplied the gate runs in \"matchers-only\" mode\n    (no covering evals to resolve), so ``coverage_result`` is still\n    populated but ``coverage_map`` will be empty.\n    \"\"\"\n    payload = report.to_json()\n    marker: dict[str, object] = {\"requested\": bool(check_coverage)}\n    if check_coverage and coverage_result is not None:\n        marker[\"status\"] = \"passed\" if coverage_result.passed else \"failed\"\n        marker[\"result\"] = coverage_result.to_dict()\n    else:\n        marker[\"status\"] = \"skipped\"\n    payload[\"coverage_gate\"] = marker\n    return payload\n\n\nHARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE = 2\n\n\ndef _format_coverage_summary(result: CoverageResult) -> str:\n    \"\"\"One-line human summary for ``doctor --check-coverage`` stdout.\"\"\"\n    if not result.matchers:\n        return \"coverage gate: no in-scope matchers (passed)\"\n    if result.passed:\n        return (\n            f\"coverage gate: {len(result.covered)}/{len(result.matchers)} \"\n            f\"matcher(s) covered (passed)\"\n        )\n    return (\n        f\"coverage gate: {len(result.missing)} of {len(result.matchers)} \"\n        f\"matcher(s) uncovered (FAILED)\"\n    )\n\n\ndef _format_coverage_missing_roster(result: CoverageResult) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the stderr artifact when the coverage gate fails.\"\"\"\n    lines = [\"eval doctor: coverage gate FAILED — uncovered matcher patterns:\"]\n    for m in result.missing:\n        lines.append(f\"  - {m.event}: {m.pattern}\")\n    return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# FR-CLI2 ``eval list`` (T01.21 / D-0018)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\n_DEFAULT_SUITES_DIR: Path = SCHEMA_PATH.parent\n\"\"\"Default location for built-in suite manifests.\n\nResolves to ``src/superclaude/cli/eval/suites/``. The directory currently\nonly ships ``suite.schema.json`` and an ``__init__.py``; built-in\n``*.yaml`` manifests will land in subsequent milestones. The glob in\n:func:`discover_suite_manifests` filters to ``*.yaml`` so the schema and\npackage marker never appear in the listing.\n\"\"\"\n\n\ndef discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir: Path) -> list[Path]:\n    \"\"\"Return ``suites_dir/*.yaml`` sorted by filename for determinism.\n\n    ``eval list`` MUST produce identical output for a given suite\n    directory across invocations (FR-CLI2 AC), so the sort is part of the\n    contract — not an implementation detail.\n\n    A missing or non-directory ``suites_dir`` is treated as the\n    empty-directory case (no suites found) rather than an error: a fresh\n    checkout that has not yet populated built-in suites must not break\n    ``eval list``.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    if not suites_dir.is_dir():\n        return []\n    return sorted(suites_dir.glob(\"*.yaml\"))\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass SuiteSummary:\n    \"\"\"One row in the ``eval list`` output.\n\n    Attributes:\n        name: Manifest ``name`` field (verbatim).\n        version: Manifest ``version`` field (verbatim, string).\n        eval_count: Number of evals after parameterize expansion. This\n            reflects \"how many evals would run\" rather than the raw\n            ``evals[]`` length, which matches operator intent.\n        source: Filesystem path of the manifest (used for human output\n            and stable JSON ordering).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    version: str\n    eval_count: int\n    source: Path\n\n    def to_dict(self) -> dict:\n        return {\n            \"name\": self.name,\n            \"version\": self.version,\n            \"eval_count\": self.eval_count,\n        }\n\n\ndef summarize_suites(\n    suites_dir: Path,\n    *,\n    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n) -> list[SuiteSummary]:\n    \"\"\"Load every ``*.yaml`` in ``suites_dir`` and project to summaries.\n\n    Any :class:`SuiteLoaderError` raised by :class:`SuiteLoader` propagates\n    so the CLI boundary can map it to\n    :data:`SUITE_LOADER_ERROR_EXIT_CODE` (= 2). The loader is injectable\n    for tests that want to stub schema/regex/capability gates without\n    touching the file system.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    loader = loader if loader is not None else SuiteLoader()\n    summaries: list[SuiteSummary] = []\n    for manifest in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n        parsed: ParsedSuite = loader.load(manifest)\n        summaries.append(\n            SuiteSummary(\n                name=parsed.name,\n                version=parsed.version,\n                eval_count=len(parsed.evals),\n                source=manifest,\n            )\n        )\n    return summaries\n\n\ndef render_list_text(summaries: Iterable[SuiteSummary]) -> str:\n    \"\"\"Render the human-readable ``eval list`` output.\"\"\"\n\n    rows = list(summaries)\n    lines = [\"superclaude eval list:\"]\n    if not rows:\n        lines.append(\"  (no suites found)\")\n        return \"\\n\".join(lines)\n    for row in rows:\n        lines.append(\n            f\"  - Exit codes:\n\n    * ``0`` — every HARD capability satisfied.\n    * ``2`` — at least one HARD capability failed, or ``--output-dir``\n      was supplied and escaped the OPS-002 / AC12 scratch-root              click.echo(f\"eval doctor: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}\", err=True)\n                sys.exit(SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE)\n            except SuiteLoaderError as exc:\n _evalspec_to_dict(spec: EvalSpec) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Project an ``EvalSpec`` into a manifest-shaped dict.\n\n    Field ordering matches ``suite.schema.json`` so YAML/JSON output is\n    deterministic and round-trips visually against the source manifest.\n    Optional fields are omitted when they hold their dataclass default\n    (e.g. ``category=\"\"``, empty tuples, ``timeout_sec=None``) so the\n    \"no editorial transformation\" guarantee from T01.22 Notes holds: an\n    eval that did not declare ``category:`` in YAML does not gain one\n    here.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    payload: dict[str, Any] = {\n        \"id\": spec.id,\n        \"title\": spec.title,\n    }\n    if spec.category:\n        payload[\"category\"] = spec.category\n    if spec.requires:\n        payload[\"requires\"] = list(spec.requires)\n    if spec.timeout_sec is not None:\n        payload[\"timeout_sec\"] = spec.timeout_sec\n    if spec.isolation is not None:\n        payload[\"isolation\"] = dict(spec.isolation)\n    if spec.inputs:\n        payload[\"inputs\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.inputs]\n    if spec.expects:\n        payload[\"expects\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.expects]\n    if spec.parameterize:\n        payload[\"parameterize\"] = [dict(row) for row in spec.parameterize]\n    if spec.no_pty is not None:\n        payload[\"no_pty\"] = spec.no_pty\n    return ``--suite <token>`` to a manifest path on disk.\n\n    Resolution precedence (first match wins):\n\n    1. ``token`` is itself a file path that exists → return it.\n    2. ``suites_dir / f\"{token}.yaml\"`` exists → return it (filename stem\n       convention used by the built-in suites).\n    3. CLI\n            callers map this to :data:`SUITE_NOT_FOUND_EXIT_CODE` (= 2).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    candidate = Path(suite)\n    if candidate.is_file():\n        return candidate\n\n    by_filename = suites_dir / f\"{suite}.yaml\"\n    if by_filename.is_file():\n        return by_filename\n\n    loader = SuiteLoader()\n    for manifest_path in discover_suite_manifests(suites_dir):\n        try:\n            parsed = loader.load(manifest_path)\n        except SuiteLoaderError:\n            # Neighbour manifest is broken; don't let it mask the lookup.\n            continue\n        if parsed.name == suite:\n            return manifest_path\n\n    raise SuiteNotFound(suite, suites_dir)\n\n\ndef describe_suite(\n    suite: str,\n    *,\n    suites_dir: Path,\n    eval_id: Optional[str] = None,\n    loader: Optional[SuiteLoader] = None,\n) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Resolve, load, and project a suite (optionally a single eval).\n\n    Validation runs via :class:`SuiteLoader` BEFORE any rendering, so\n    schema / id-regex / capability rejections raise before the caller\n    can produce stdout (FR-CLI3 AC: validate before print).\n\n    Args:\n        suite: Suite name, filename stem, or direct manifest path.        return _parsed_suite_to_dict(parsed)\n\n    for spec in parsed.evals:\n        if spec.id == eval_id:\n            return _evalspec_to_dict(spec)\n    raise EvalNotFound(\n Failures\n145\t       inside the callback are swallowed — signal handlers must always\n146\t       return cleanly because the runtime may re-enter Python at a\n147\t       restricted point. Use the callback for *bookkeeping only*\n148\t       (logging, writing a partial-summary stub); the heavy work of\n149\t       writing the final partial summary belongs in the orchestrator\n150\t       once the main thread returns from its blocking join.\n151\t\n152\t    On ``__exit__`` the previous handlers (captured on entry) are\n153\t    restored. Installing twice nests cleanly: the inner installer\n154\t    captures the outer installer's handler and restores it on exit.\n155\t\n156\t    Limitations\n157\t    -----------\n158\t\n159\t    Python's :func:`signal.signal` may only be called from the main\n160\t    thread of the main interpreter. The\nper-eval timeout path inside :class:`EvalRunner` is responsible for\nkilling the underlying :class:`PtyDriver` subprocess and reaping its\nzombie; the signal handler only flips the cooperative flag so the\norchestrator can stop scheduling new evals and the in-flight ones can be\nclassified as ``INTERRUPTED`` once they exit (whether cleanly or via the\nsubprocess kill).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport signal\nimport threading\nfrom types import FrameType\nfrom typing import Callable, Iterable, Optional\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"EXIT_INTERRUPTED\",\n    \"DEFAULT_INTERRUPT_SIGNALS\",\n    \"CancellationToken\",\n    \"SignalHandlerInstaller\",\n]\n\n\n# Per design-spec §4 (Exit codes table) and NFR-REL1: SIGINT / SIGTERM\n# during a run terminates with exit code 3. The constant lives here so\n# the orchestrator (T03.15) and the CLI dispatcher (Phase 4) import a\n# single source of truth.\nEXIT_INTERRUPTED: int = 3\n\n\n# ``SIGINT`` and ``SIGTERM`` are both wired to the same cancellation\n# action per design-spec §12 \"Signal handling\". ``SIGINT`` is the Ctrl-C\n# path; ``SIGTERM`` is the supervisor / container path.\nDEFAULT_INTERRUPT_SIGNALS: tuple[int, ...] = (signal.SIGINT, signal.SIGTERM)\n\n\nclass CancellationToken:\n    \"\"\"Thread-safe cooperative cancellation flag.\n\n    Wraps :class:`threading.Event` so producers can ``cancel()`` the\n    token from the signal handler (or from a unit test) and consumers\n    can poll ``is_cancelled()`` or block on ``wait(timeout)``. The flag\n    is one-shot — once cancelled the token stays cancelled for the rest\n    of its lifetime; the harness allocates a fresh token per run rather\n    than resetting an in-use one. The optional ``signum_holder``\n    attribute records the signal number that triggered cancellation so\n    downstream consumers can include it in the partial summary.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    __slots__ = (\"_event\", \"_signum\", \"_signum_lock\")\n\n    def __init__(self) -> None:\n        self._event = threading.Event()\n        self._signum: Optional[int] = None\n        self._signum_lock = threading.Lock()\n\n    def cancel(self, *, signum: Optional[int] = None) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Flip the token to the cancelled state.\n\n        Parameters\n        ----------\n        signum:\n            Optional signal number that caused the cancellation. Stored\n            on the token so the orchestrator / reporter can record\n            ``\"interrupted_by\"`` in the partial summary. Only the first\n            ``cancel()`` call captures the signum; subsequent calls\n            return ``False`` without overwriting.\n\n        Returns\n        -------\n        bool\n            ``True`` if this call was the one that flipped the flag,\n            ``False`` if the token was already cancelled. Lets the\n            orchestrator distinguish \"I cancelled\" from \"I observed\n            an already-cancelled token\" without racing against a\n            second signal.\n        \"\"\"\n        first = not self._event.is_set()\n        if first:\n            with self._signum_lock:\n                if not self._event.is_set():\n                    if signum is not None:\n                        self._signum = int(signum)\n                    self._event.set()\n                else:\n                    first = False\n        return first\n\n    def is_cancelled(self) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return ``True`` once the token has been cancelled.\"\"\"\n        return self._event.is_set()\n\n    def wait(self, timeout: Optional[float] = None) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Block until cancelled or ``timeout`` elapses.\n\n        Returns ``True`` if the token is cancelled when the call\n        returns, ``False`` on timeout. Matches the\n        :meth:`threading.Event.wait` contract so callers familiar with\n        ``Event`` semantics need no adapter.\n        \"\"\"\n        return self._event.wait(timeout)\n\n    @property\n    def signum(self) -> Optional[int]:\n        \"\"\"The signal number that caused cancellation, if any.\"\"\"\n        with self._signum_lock:\n            return self._signum\n\n\n# Signal-handler callback signature: ``(signum, frame, token)`` — matches\n# the stdlib :class:`signal.signal` callback plus the token so callers\n# can implement extra bookkeeping (partial-summary writing, telemetry)\n# without keeping a global token reference.\nOnSignalCallback = Callable[[int, Optional[FrameType], \"CancellationToken\"], None]\n\n\nclass SignalHandlerInstaller:\n    \"\"\"Context manager binding ``SIGINT``/``SIGTERM`` to a cancellation token.\n\n    On ``__enter__`` the installer registers a handler for each signal\n    in ``signals`` that:\n\n    1. Calls ``token.cancel(signum=...)`` so cooperative consumers wake\n       up immediately.\n    2. Invokes the optional ``on_signal`` callback if provided. Failures\n       inside the callback are swallowed — signal handlers must always\n       return cleanly because the runtime may re-enter Python at a\n       restricted point. Use the callback for *bookkeeping only*\n       (logging, writing a partial-summary stub); the heavy work of\n       writing the final partial summary belongs in the orchestrator\n       once the main thread returns from its blocking join.\n\n    On ``__exit__`` the previous handlers (captured on entry) are\n    restored. Installing twice nests cleanly: the inner installer\n    captures the outer installer's handler and restores it on exit.\n\n    Limitations\n    -----------\n\n    Python's :func:`signal.signal` may only be called from the main\n    thread of the main interpreter. Constructing this installer outside\n    the main thread raises :class:`ValueError`. Tests that exercise the\n    handler path must run on the main thread or substitute a stub.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(\n        self,\n        token: CancellationToken,\n        *,\n        signals: Iterable[int] = DEFAULT_INTERRUPT_SIGNALS,\n        on_signal: Optional[OnSignalCallback] = None,\n    ) -> None:\n        self._token = token\n        self._signals: tuple[int, ...] = tuple(int(s) for s in signals)\n        if not self._signals:\n            raise ValueError(\"at least one signal number is required\")\n        self._on_signal = on_signal\n        self._previous: dict[int, object] = {}\n        self._installed = False\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Context-manager surface\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def __enter__(self) -> \"SignalHandlerInstaller\":\n        self.install()\n        return self\n\n    def __exit__(self, exc_type, exc, tb) -> None:\n        self.restore()\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Explicit install / restore (for callers that cannot use ``with``)\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def install(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Bind the handler for every configured signal.\n\n        Idempotent: calling install twice is a no-op on the second call\n        (the previous-handler snapshot is taken once).\n        \"\"\"\n        if self._installed:\n            return\n        if threading.current_thread() is not threading.main_thread():\n            raise ValueError(\n                \"SignalHandlerInstaller must be installed from the main thread\"\n            )\n        for signum in self._signals:\n            prev = signal.signal(signum, self._make_handler())\n            self._previous[signum] = prev\n        self._installed = True\n\n    def restore(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Restore the previous handler for every configured signal.\n\n        Idempotent: safe to call without a prior ``install()``. If\n        :func:`signal.signal` fails (e.g. the previous handler captured\n        a C-level extension that cannot be re-registered from Python)\n        the per-signal failure is swallowed so the harness shutdown\n        path stays clean.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not self._installed:\n            return\n        for signum in self._signals:\n            prev = self._previous.get(signum, signal.SIG_DFL)\n            try:\n                signal.signal(signum, prev)  # type: ignore[arg-type]\n            except (ValueError, OSError):  # pragma: no cover - defensive\n                # Restoration is best-effort; failure here cannot block\n                # shutdown.\n                pass\n        self._previous.clear()\n        self._installed = False\n\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n    # Internals\n    # ------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n    def _make_handler(self) -> Callable[[int, Optional[FrameType]], None]:\n        token = self._token\n        on_signal = self._on_signal\n\n        def _handler(signum: int, frame: Optional[FrameType]) -> None:\n            token.cancel(signum=signum)\n            if on_signal is None:\n                return\n            try:\n                on_signal(signum, frame, token)\n            except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 — signal handlers must not raise\n                # Bookkeeping callbacks are best-effort; swallow any\n                # failure so the runtime never re-raises from inside the\n                # signal handler context.\n                return\n\n        return _handler\n","numLines":255,"startLine":1,"totalLines":255}}}

Four FR-RPT1 +\nDM-012 obligations have to hold for any caller that turns a\n:class:`RunSummary` into bytes:\n\n1. **N'-vs-K equality** — ``len(summary.evals) ==\n   counts.expanded_n_prime``. A summary that satisfies the dimensional\n   invariant must render through every emitter (``to_markdown``,\n   ``to_json``, ``to_yaml``, ``to_junit``) without raising.\n\n2. **SKIPPED inclusion** — design-spec §9 declares that SKIPPED rows\n   stay in ``evals[]`` (with ``skip_reason`` populated) so the row\n   accounting never tries to \"compress\" the array. The JSON the\n   Reporter writes must surface those rows verbatim — both at the row\n   level (``evals[].status == \"SKIPPED\"`` with ``skip_reason``) and at\n   the count level (``counts.skipped_s`` reflects the tally).\n\n3. **Mismatch failure → exit code 2** — when the invariant is\n   violated, every emitter raises :class:`ReporterContractViolation`\n   *before* writing any artefact, and the violation maps to process\n   exit code 2 (``REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE``). ),\n    )\n\n\ndef _summary(\n    *,\n    evals: tuple[EvalOutcome, ...],\n    expanded_n_prime: int | None = None,\n    kept_k: int | None = None,\n    skipped_s: int | None = None,\n) -> Every single-format emitter on the Reporter wrapper raises.\n    reporter = Reporter(summary, emit_junit=True)\n    for emitter_name in (\"to_markdown\", \"to_json\", \"to_yaml\", \"to_junit\"):\n        with pytest.raises(ReporterContractViolation):\n            getattr(reporter, emitter_name)()\n\n    # Diagnostic shape on the exception itself.\n    err = exc_info.value\n    assert err.expected == 3\n    assert err.actual == 2\n    assert err.run_id == \"run-test007\"\n    msg = str(err)\n    assert \"len(evals)=2\" in msg\n    assert \"expanded_n_prime=3\" in msg\n    assert \"run-test007\" in msg\n\n    # Goal\n13\t\n14\tPin the four load-bearing TEST-007 obligations the Reporter (COMP-008)\n15\tmust satisfy for downstream consumers (CI summary jobs, the JUnit\n16\tpipeline, the `superclaude eval run` exit-code dispatcher). The Reporter\n17\tis the single point where the orchestrator's accounting becomes\n18\tmachine-readable bytes; if its contract drifts, every downstream consumer\n19\tsilently inherits the drift. These tests stand as the canonical\n20\tregression line.\n21\t\n22\t## 2. Test matrix\n23\t\n24\tThe four pytest cases in `tests/cli/eval/test_reporter_contract.py`\n25\tcorrespond one-to-one with the TEST-007 scenarios named in the phase-4\n26\ttasklist:\n27\t\n28\t| # | Scenario | Test function | Pins |\n29\t|---|---|---|---|\n30\t| 1 | **N'-vs-K equality** | `test_n_prime_equals_k_lets_every_emitter_render` | When `len(summary.evals) == counts.expanded_n_prime`, every emitter (`to_markdown`, `to_json`, `to_yaml`, `to_junit`) renders without raising; `Reporter.write(...)` drops the canonical four-file artefact set; JUnit `<testsuite tests=\"...\">` mirrors `expanded_n_prime`. |\n31\t| 2 | **SKIPPED inclusion** | `test_skipped_rows_included_in_evals_with_skip_reason` | SKIPPED rows survive in `summary.json :: evals[]` with `status=\"SKIPPED\"`, `skip_reason` and `skip_flag_triggered` populated; per-status `totals.skipped` and `counts.skipped_s` reflect the tally; the markdown table surfaces the same rows + reasons. |\n32\t| 3 | **Mismatch → exit code 2** | `test_n_prime_vs_k_mismatch_raises_and_maps_to_exit_code_two` | A mismatched summary (`len(evals) != counts.expanded_n_prime`) raises `ReporterContractViolation` from every emitter and from `write_aggregated_report` *before* any artefact is written to disk; `REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE == 2`; the exception carries `expected`, `actual`, and `run_id` plus a diagnostic message including both sides of the mismatch. |\n33\t| 4 | **JSON schema fidelity** | `test_reporter_json_validates_against_summary_schema` | The JSON the Reporter renders validates against `summary.schema.json` (DM-012 / T03.10) on *both* the in-memory string from `Reporter.to_json()` and the bytes the writer drops on disk via `Reporter.write(...)`; both payloads compare equal so a refactor cannot divergence them. The fixture covers every distinct row class (PASS / SKIPPED / FAIL / ERRORED) so every `oneOf` branch in the schema is exercised. |\n34\t\n35\t## 3. Fixture design\n36\t\n37\t`_summary(evals=..., expanded_n_prime=..., kept_k=..., skipped_s=...)`\n38\tauto-derives the counts when overrides are not supplied. Overriding\n39\t`expanded_n_prime` to a value other than `len(evals)` is the canonical\n40\tway to construct the mismatch fixture: the Reporter sees\n41\t`len(evals) != counts.expanded_n_prime` and raises.\n42\t\n43\tFixtures are intentionally self-contained — they do not reuse\n44\t`test_run_report.py` helpers — so a refactor of the writer helpers\n45\tcannot quietly drop the contract guarantees this module pins.\n46\t\n47\t## 4. ``2`` — harness contract error: invalid flag, scratch-root violation,\n  suite-not-found, disk-budget exceeded, Reporter contract violation,\n  capability gate breach, etc. The harness rejected the run before /\n  during dispatch.\n* ``3`` — interrupted: SIGINT / SIGTERM landed mid-run and the\n  cooperative cancellation path drained.\n\nSibling tests:\n\n* :mod:`tests.cli.eval.test_reporter_contract` (T04.17) pins the same\n  exit-code-2 contract at the *library* boundary\n  (``REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE`` constant + exception\n  type). This module pins the same number through the process boundary\n  so the constant cannot drift without breaking the operator-visible\n  surface.\n\nForward-compat:\n\nThe exit-code-2 paths (``--timeout-mult <=0``, ``--max-disk-mb <0``)\nare guarded by flag validation that runs BEFORE the\n:mod:`superclaude.cli.eval.commands` run-id helper, so they exercise\nreal ``sys.exit(HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE)`` calls today. The 0 / 1 / 3 paths\nall flow through the run-loop closure whose helpers (``_new_run_id``,\n``_run_one_spec``, ``_compute_run_stats``, ``RUN_CLEAN_EXIT_CODE``,\n``RUN_FAILURES_EXIT_CODE``, ``RUN_INTERRUPTED_EXIT_CODE``) are T04.10\ndeliverables. While those helpers are absent we ``pytest.skip`` those\nthree tests with a self-clearing diagnostic; the skips evaporate once\nT04.10 lands, so the suite then pins all four codes end-to-end.\n\nThe exit-code-2 test always runs because its contract is the one this\nrelease can prove today and is the one the Reporter / Capability /\nSuite-loader contracts already lean on.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport os\nimport signal\nimport subprocess\nimport sys\nimport time\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport pytest\n\n\nREPO_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]\n\n\ndef _resolve_superclaude_bin() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Locate the ``superclaude`` console-script in the active venv.\n\n    The package has no ``__main__.py``, so ``python -m superclaude``\n    fails. The console-script entry generated by hatchling is the\n    canonical operator surface — and is exactly what CI / docs tell\n    users to invoke.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    venv_bin = Path(sys.executable).parent / \"superclaude\"\n    if venv_bin.exists():\n        return str(venv_bin)\n    import shutil\n\n    path_bin = shutil.which(\"superclaude\")\n    if path_bin:\n        return path_bin\n    pytest.skip(\n        \"superclaude console-script not found on this environment; \"\n        \"exit-code tests need the operator-visible CLI shim.\"\n    )\n\n\nSUPERCLAUDE_BIN = _resolve_superclaude_bin()\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Forward-dependency probe (T04.10 helpers)\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _t0410_missing() -> list[str]:\n    \"\"\"Return the names of T04.10 run-loop deliverables not yet defined.\n\n    Tests for exit codes 0, 1, and 3 traverse the run-loop closure;\n    that closure references helpers defined in T04.10. When any of\n    them are missing the closure raises ``NameError`` before reaching\n    the exit-code branch, so we skip those tests with a diagnostic\n    that auto-clears once the helpers land.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    from superclaude.cli.eval import commands as _commands_mod\n\n    forward_deps = (\n        \"_new_run_id\",\n        \"_run_one_spec\",\n        \"_compute_run_stats\",\n        \"RUN_CLEAN_EXIT_CODE\",\n        \"RUN_FAILURES_EXIT_CODE\",\n        \"RUN_INTERRUPTED_EXIT_CODE\",\n    )\n    return [n for n in forward_deps if not hasattr(_commands_mod, n)]\n\n\ndef _skip_unless_t0410_landed() -> None:\n    missing = _t0410_missing()\n    if missing:\n        pytest.skip(\n            f\"T04.10 forward dependency not yet landed: {missing!r}. \"\n            \"The exit-code-2 path pinned below runs today; the 0 / 1 / 3 \"\n            \"paths un-skip automatically once T04.10 wires the run-loop \"\n            \"closure helpers.\"\n        )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# subprocess helper\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef _run_eval(\n    *args: str,\n    timeout: float = 60.0,\n    env: dict[str, str] | None = None,\n) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:\n    \"\"\"Invoke ``superclaude eval run ...`` as a real subprocess.\n\n    Uses ``sys.executable -m superclaude`` to avoid depending on the\n    PATH-installed shim, matching how CI pipelines run the harness.\n    Captures stdout + stderr and returns the completed process so the\n    caller can assert on both the exit code and the surfaced\n    diagnostic.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    cmd = [\n        sys.executable,\n        \"-m\",\n        \"superclaude\",\n        \"eval\",\n        \"run\",\n        *args,\n    ]\n    merged_env = dict(os.environ)\n    if env:\n        merged_env.update(env)\n    return subprocess.run(\n        cmd,\n        capture_output=True,\n        text=True,\n        cwd=str(REPO_ROOT),\n        env=merged_env,\n        timeout=timeout,\n        check=False,\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Exit code 0 — clean run\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_exit_code_0_clean_run(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"``--suite real --no-pty`` skips every E1-E15 → clean exit 0.\n\n    The DOC-OQ3 / D-0077 exclusion set tags every eval in ``real.yaml``\n    ``no_pty: skip``. With ``--no-pty`` on the CLI the orchestrator\n    short-circuits the whole suite into SKIPPED outcomes — no failures,\n    no breaches, so design-spec §4 lands at exit ``0``.\n\n    Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (see module\n    docstring).\n    \"\"\"\n\n    _skip_unless_t0410_landed()\n\n    output_dir = tmp_path / \"run\"\n    result = _run_eval(\n        \"--suite\",\n        \"real\",\n        \"--no-pty\",\n        \"--output-dir\",\n        str(output_dir),\n    )\n\n    assert result.returncode == 0, (\n        f\"clean run must exit 0, got {result.returncode}\\n\"\n        f\"--- stdout ---\\n{result.stdout}\\n--- stderr ---\\n{result.stderr}\"\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Exit code 1 — failing run\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_exit_code_1_failing_run(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"A run with at least one FAIL outcome exits 1.\n\n    Authored against a synthetic single-eval suite whose body is wired\n    to fail its ``expects`` block. Since the body wiring depends on\n    T04.10's ``_run_one_spec`` we skip until that helper lands; the\n    library-level FAIL → exit-1 mapping is already pinned by the\n    Reporter and run-report contract tests.\n\n    When T04.10 lands this test will:\n\n    1. Materialise a tmp suite with one spec that fails by construction.\n    2. Run ``superclaude eval run --suite <tmp> --no-pty`` (or with the\n       synthetic body inline).\n    3. Assert ``returncode == 1`` and the summary records the failure.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    _skip_unless_t0410_landed()\n\n    # Synthetic single-eval suite that fails by construction. We rely\n    # on a deliberately mismatched ``expect:`` so the run finishes\n    # cleanly with one FAIL outcome rather than a harness breach.\n    suite_dir = tmp_path / \"suite\"\n    suite_dir.mkdir(parents=True)\n    suite_path = suite_dir / \"fail.yaml\"\n    suite_path.write_text(\n        \"name: fail\\n\"\n        \"version: '1.0'\\n\"\n        \"description: 'one-eval suite that fails by construction'\\n\"\n        \"defaults:\\n\"\n        \"  per_eval_timeout_sec: 30\\n\"\n        \"  per_eval_memory_mb: 256\\n\"\n        \"  capture_tty: false\\n\"\n        \"evals:\\n\"\n        \"  - id: F1\\n\"\n        \"    title: 'fail by construction'\\n\"\n        \"    category: synthetic\\n\"\n        \"    timeout_sec: 30\\n\"\n        \"    isolation:\\n\"\n        \"      home_strategy: ephemeral\\n\"\n        \"    inputs: []\\n\"\n        \"    expects:\\n\"\n        \"      - kind: exit_code\\n\"\n        \"        value: 9999\\n\"\n    )\n\n    output_dir = tmp_path / \"run\"\n    result = _run_eval(\n        \"--suite\",\n        str(suite_path),\n        \"--no-pty\",\n        \"--output-dir\",\n        str(output_dir),\n    )\n\n    # `--no-pty` may short-circuit a tagged suite to SKIPPED. Without\n    # ``no_pty: skip`` on the spec the runner enters the FAIL path and\n    # the suite returns 1.\n    assert result.returncode == 1, (\n        f\"failing run must exit 1, got {result.returncode}\\n\"\n        f\"--- stdout ---\\n{result.stdout}\\n--- stderr ---\\n{result.stderr}\"\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Exit code 2 — harness contract error\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_exit_code_2_harness_error(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"Invalid flags trigger ``HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE`` (= 2).\n\n    Pins the contract through two independent harness-error gates so\n    the exit-code-2 surface is verified from more than one entry\n    point:\n\n    1. ``--timeout-mult 0`` — flag validation at\n       ``commands.py:1398`` raises ``HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE``.\n    2. ``--max-disk-mb -1`` — flag validation at\n       ``commands.py:1405`` raises ``HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE``.\n\n    Both gates run BEFORE the run-loop closure, so this test is\n    independent of T04.10 and runs today.\n\n    The test also asserts the harness's exit-code-2 constant equals\n    ``2`` at the library boundary; together with the process-level\n    assertion that guards against the constant drifting away from the\n    contract that operators and CI consume.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    from superclaude.cli.eval.commands import HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE\n\n    # Library-boundary constant pin — guards against the constant\n    # drifting silently away from design-spec §4.\n    assert HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE == 2, (\n        f\"HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE must equal 2 per design-spec §4, \"\n        f\"got {HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE}\"\n    )\n\n    output_dir = tmp_path / \"run\"\n\n    # Gate 1 — --timeout-mult must be > 0.\n    timeout_result = _run_eval(\n        \"--suite\",\n        \"real\",\n        \"--no-pty\",\n        \"--timeout-mult\",\n        \"0\",\n        \"--output-dir\",\n        str(output_dir / \"tm\"),\n    )\n    assert timeout_result.returncode == 2, (\n        \"invalid --timeout-mult must exit 2 (HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE); \"\n        f\"got {timeout_result.returncode}\\n\"\n        f\"--- stdout ---\\n{timeout_result.stdout}\\n\"\n        f\"--- stderr ---\\n{timeout_result.stderr}\"\n    )\n    assert \"--timeout-mult\" in timeout_result.stderr, (\n        \"stderr must surface the validated flag name \"\n        f\"(--timeout-mult); got:\\n{timeout_result.stderr}\"\n    )\n\n    # Gate 2 — --max-disk-mb must be >= 0.\n    disk_result = _run_eval(\n        \"--suite\",\n        \"real\",\n        \"--no-pty\",\n        \"--max-disk-mb\",\n        \"-1\",\n        \"--output-dir\",\n        str(output_dir / \"mdmb\"),\n    )\n    assert disk_result.returncode == 2, (\n        \"invalid --max-disk-mb must exit 2 (HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE); \"\n        f\"got {disk_result.returncode}\\n\"\n        f\"--- stdout ---\\n{disk_result.stdout}\\n\"\n        f\"--- stderr ---\\n{disk_result.stderr}\"\n    )\n    assert \"--max-disk-mb\" in disk_result.stderr, (\n        \"stderr must surface the validated flag name \"\n        f\"(--max-disk-mb); got:\\n{disk_result.stderr}\"\n    )\n\n\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n# Exit code 3 — interrupted run\n# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------\n\n\ndef test_exit_code_3_interrupted_run(tmp_path: Path) -> None:\n    \"\"\"SIGINT mid-run lands at ``RUN_INTERRUPTED_EXIT_CODE`` (= 3).\n\n    Spawns ``superclaude eval run`` in a child process, gives the\n    orchestrator a moment to install its signal handlers (NFR-REL1 /\n    T03.07), then delivers ``SIGINT``. The cooperative cancellation\n    path drains and the child exits with code 3.\n\n    Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (the signal\n    handler is installed inside that closure; without it the child\n    raises NameError on ``_new_run_id`` before reaching the wait\n    state). The signal-handler contract itself is pinned by\n    :mod:`tests.cli.eval.test_signal_handling`; this test pins the\n    process-level mapping from a delivered signal to exit code 3.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    _skip_unless_t0410_landed()\n\n    # Library-boundary constant pin — guards against drift away from\n    # design-spec §4.\n    from superclaude.cli.eval.signal_handler import EXIT_INTERRUPTED\n\n    assert EXIT_INTERRUPTED == 3, (\n        f\"EXIT_INTERRUPTED must equal 3 per design-spec §4, \"\n        f\"got {EXIT_INTERRUPTED}\"\n    )\n\n    output_dir = tmp_path / \"run\"\n    cmd = [\n        sys.executable,\n        \"-m\",\n        \"superclaude\",\n        \"eval\",\n        \"run\",\n        \"--suite\",\n        \"real\",\n        \"--output-dir\",\n        str(output_dir),\n    ]\n\n    proc = subprocess.Popen(\n        cmd,\n        stdout=subprocess.PIPE,\n        stderr=subprocess.PIPE,\n        text=True,\n        cwd=str(REPO_ROOT),\n    )\n\n    # Give the orchestrator time to install its signal handlers before\n    # delivering SIGINT. We rely\n    # on a deliberately mismatched ``expect:`` so the run finishes\n    # cleanly with one FAIL outcome rather than a harness breach.\n    suite_dir = tmp_path / \"suite\"\n    suite_dir.mkdir(parents=True)\n    suite_path = suite_dir / \"fail.yaml\"\n    suite_path.write_text(\n        \"name: fail\\n\"\n        \"version: '1.0'\\n\"\n        \"description: 'one-eval suite that fails by construction'\\n\"\n        \"defaults:\\n\"\n        \"  per_eval_timeout_sec: 30\\n\"\n        \"  per_eval_memory_mb: 256\\n\"\n        \"  capture_tty: false\\n\"\n        \"evals:\\n\"\n        \"  - id: F1\\n\"\n        \"    title: 'fail by construction'\\n\"\n        \"    category: synthetic\\n\"\n        \"    timeout_sec: 30\\n\"\n        \"    isolation:\\n\"\n        \"      home_strategy: ephemeral\\n\"\n        \"    inputs: []\\n\"\n        \"    expects:\\n\"\n        \"      - kind: exit_code\\n\"\n        \"        value: 9999\\n\"\n    )\n\n    output_dir = tmp_path / \"run\"\n    result = _run_eval(\n        \"--suite\",\n        str(suite_path),\n        \"--no-pty\",\n        \"--output-dir\",\n        str(output_dir),\n    )\n\n    # `--no-pty` may short-circuit a tagged suite to SKIPPED. Goal\n\nPin the four exit codes named in design-spec §4 at the **process\nboundary** that operators, CI jobs, and downstream pipelines actually\nread (subprocess `returncode`). The library boundary already has its\nown pins — `REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` is held by the\nT04.17 / D-0078 contract suite; `EXIT_INTERRUPTED` is held by the\nT03.07 / D-0050 signal-handler suite — but neither of those verifies\nthat the constant actually flows out through `sys.exit` and into the\nshell. This test module closes that gap, so a future refactor that\ndrops the constant on the way to `sys.exit` (or maps it to the wrong\nexit branch) fails loudly here rather than silently in CI summary\nparsers.\n\n## 2. Test matrix\n\nThe four pytest cases in `tests/cli/eval/test_exit_codes.py`\ncorrespond one-to-one with the TEST-008 scenarios named in the phase-4\ntasklist:\n\n| # | Scenario | Test function | Pins |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **Clean run** | `test_exit_code_0_clean_run` | `superclaude eval run --suite real --no-pty --output-dir <tmp>` exits `0`. Every E1-E15 spec is tagged `no_pty: skip` (DOC-OQ3 / D-0077), so `--no-pty` short-circuits the whole suite into SKIPPED outcomes — no failures, no breaches, exit `0`. Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (see §4). |\n| 2 | **Failing run** | `test_exit_code_1_failing_run` | A synthetic single-eval suite whose `expect:` block is wired to fail is run via `superclaude eval run`; the orchestrator completes cleanly with one FAIL outcome, so the dispatcher exits `1` per design-spec §4. Skipped until T04.10 wires `_run_one_spec`. |\n| 3 | **Harness contract error** | `test_exit_code_2_harness_error` | Two independent flag-validation gates (`--timeout-mult 0` and `--max-disk-mb -1`) each surface `eval run: --<flag> ...` on stderr and exit `2`. Both gates run BEFORE the run-loop closure, so this test runs today and is the canonical pin for the operator-visible exit-`2` contract. The test also pins `HARD_FAIL_EXIT_CODE == 2` at the library boundary so a constant rename cannot drift the contract silently. |\n| 4 | **Interrupted run** | `test_exit_code_3_interrupted_run` | `subprocess.Popen` spawns the run, gives the orchestrator 2 s to install signal handlers, then delivers `SIGINT`. The cooperative cancellation path (NFR-REL1 / T03.07) drains and the child exits with code `3`. The test also pins `EXIT_INTERRUPTED == 3` at the library boundary. Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (the signal handler is installed inside that closure). |\n\n## 3. subprocess invocation\n\n`_run_eval(...)` invokes the **console-script** at\n`<venv>/bin/superclaude`, not `python -m superclaude`. The package\ndeliberately ships without a `__main__.py`; the console-script\ngenerated by hatchling is the canonical operator surface and matches\nhow CI / docs tell users to invoke the harness. The helper resolves\nthe binary off `sys.executable`'s parent so the test stays correct in\nany venv layout (CI, `make dev`, ephemeral worktrees) and skips\ngracefully if no console-script is installed on the host.\n\n## 4. Forward-dependency policy (T04.10 deferral)\n\nThe 0 / 1 / 3 paths all flow through the run-loop closure whose\nhelpers (`_new_run_id`, `_run_one_spec`, `_compute_run_stats`,\n`RUN_CLEAN_EXIT_CODE`, `RUN_FAILURES_EXIT_CODE`,\n`RUN_INTERRUPTED_EXIT_CODE`) are T04.10 deliverables. While those\nhelpers are absent the closure raises `NameError` before reaching the\nexit-code branch under test. Mirroring the\n`test_no_pty_exclusion.py` pattern (T04.16), each affected test calls\n`_skip_unless_t0410_landed()`, which returns the missing names and\nissues `pytest.skip(...)` with a self-clearing diagnostic.\n\nThe exit-code-2 test **always runs**. Goal\n\nPin the four exit codes named in design-spec §4 at the **process\nboundary** that operators, CI jobs, and downstream pipelines actually\nread (subprocess `returncode`). The library boundary already has its\nown pins — `REPORTER_CONTRACT_VIOLATION_EXIT_CODE` is held by the\nT04.17 / D-0078 contract suite; `EXIT_INTERRUPTED` is held by the\nT03.07 / D-0050 signal-handler suite — but neither of those verifies\nthat the constant actually flows out through `sys.exit` and into the\nshell. Test matrix\n\nThe four pytest cases in `tests/cli/eval/test_exit_codes.py`\ncorrespond one-to-one with the TEST-008 scenarios named in the phase-4\ntasklist:\n\n| # | Scenario | Test function | Pins |\n|---|---|---|---|\n| 1 | **Clean run** | `test_exit_code_0_clean_run` | `superclaude eval run --suite real --no-pty --output-dir <tmp>` exits `0`. Every E1-E15 spec is tagged `no_pty: skip` (DOC-OQ3 / D-0077), so `--no-pty` short-circuits the whole suite into SKIPPED outcomes — no failures, no breaches, exit `0`. Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (see §4). |\n| 2 | **Failing run** | `test_exit_code_1_failing_run` | A synthetic single-eval suite whose `expect:` block is wired to fail is run via `superclaude eval run`; the orchestrator completes cleanly with one FAIL outcome, so the dispatcher exits `1` per design-spec §4. The cooperative cancellation path (NFR-REL1 / T03.07) drains and the child exits with code `3`. The test also pins `EXIT_INTERRUPTED == 3` at the library boundary. Skipped until T04.10 wires the run-loop closure (the signal handler is installed inside that closure). |\n\n## 3. subprocess invocation\n\n`_run_eval(...)` invokes the **console-script** at\n`<venv>/bin/superclaude`, not `python -m superclaude`. The package\ndeliberately ships without a `__main__.py`; the console-script\ngenerated by hatchling is the canonical operator surface and matches\nhow CI / docs tell users to invoke the harness. The helper resolves\nthe binary off `sys.executable`'s parent so the test stays correct in\nany venv layout (CI, `make dev`, ephemeral worktrees) and skips\ngracefully if no console-script is installed on the host.\n\n## 4.
